TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: build thousandths with base-ten blocks (the big cube as 1 whole, a
  flat as a tenth, a rod as a hundredth, a small cube as a thousandth); use a
  place value chart with a column for the point; weigh things on a kitchen scale
  in grams and write them in kg; always estimate first so a misplaced point is
  caught; use squared paper to line up the points. Harder: decimals with 4
  places, long chains of operations (2.5 × 0.4 + 1.25 ÷ 0.5), finding missing
  digits in decimal sums, shopping bills with quantities like 1.250 kg at ₹64.80
  per kg, and checking every division by multiplying back.


================================================================================
TOPIC 5.9 — CONVERTING FRACTIONS ↔ DECIMALS; TERMINATING VS REPEATING DECIMALS
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - A fraction is a division: 3/8 means 3 ÷ 8. So every fraction can be written
    as a decimal.
  - METHOD 1 (equivalent fractions): if the denominator divides 10, 100 or 1000,
    make that denominator: 3/4 = 75/100 = 0.75, 3/8 = 375/1000 = 0.375, 7/20 =
    35/100 = 0.35.
  - METHOD 2 (division): divide the numerator by the denominator, writing zeros
    after the point: 13 ÷ 8 = 1.625.
  - DECIMAL TO FRACTION: the last decimal place gives the denominator (tenths,
    hundredths, thousandths), then simplify with the HCF: 0.45 = 45/100 = 9/20;
    2.125 = 2 125/1000 = 2 1/8.
  - Know these by heart: 1/2 = 0.5, 1/4 = 0.25, 3/4 = 0.75, 1/5 = 0.2, 1/8 =
    0.125, 3/8 = 0.375, 5/8 = 0.625, 7/8 = 0.875, 1/10 = 0.1, 1/20 = 0.05, 1/25
    = 0.04, 1/3 = 0.(3), 2/3 = 0.(6), 1/6 = 0.1(6), 1/9 = 0.(1), 1/11 = 0.(09).
  - TERMINATING decimals stop (3/8 = 0.375). RECURRING (repeating) decimals go
    on for ever with a repeating block (1/3 = 0.333...). In this file we write
    the repeating block in brackets: 0.333... = 0.(3), 0.1666... = 0.1(6),
    0.142857142857... = 0.(142857). Many books put a dot or a bar over the
    repeating digits instead.
  - THE RULE: write the fraction in simplest form. If the denominator has no
    prime factors other than 2 and 5, the decimal terminates; if it has any
    other prime factor (3, 7, 11, ...), it recurs. This is because 10 = 2 × 5.
  - In long division by n there are at most n − 1 different non-zero remainders,
    so a remainder must come back and the digits repeat.
  - To ORDER fractions and decimals together, change them all to decimals (to 3
    places), then compare place by place.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A recipe needs 3/4 kg of flour. The shop's scale shows decimals. What should
    the scale show?
    Answer: 0.75 kg. 3/4 = 75/100 = 0.75.
    Teacher explains: A decimal is a fraction with denominator 10, 100 or 1000.
    Can 4 go into 100? Yes: 4 × 25 = 100. So multiply the top and bottom by 25:
    3/4 = 75/100, and 75 hundredths is written 0.75. Think of money: 3/4 of a
    rupee is 75 paise = ₹0.75. Always multiply the numerator by the SAME number
    as the denominator. Now you try: 7/25 kg. (28/100 = 0.28 kg)

D2. Write 3/8 as a decimal in two ways.
            0.3 7 5
           --------
        8 ) 3.0 0 0
          − 2 4
            ---
              6 0
            − 5 6
              ---
                4 0
              − 4 0
                ---
                  0
    Answer: 3/8 = 375/1000 = 0.375.
    Teacher explains: Way 1: 8 does not go into 10 or 100, but 8 × 125 = 1000.
    So 3/8 = (3 × 125)/(8 × 125) = 375/1000 = 0.375. Way 2: divide 3 by 8. 3 ÷ 8
    = 0 R 3, so write 0 and the point, then bring down zeros: 30 ÷ 8 = 3 R 6, 60
    ÷ 8 = 7 R 4, 40 ÷ 8 = 5 R 0. The remainder is 0, so the decimal stops:
    0.375. Both ways agree. Learn the eighths: 1/8 = 0.125, and each eighth adds
    0.125. Now you try: 5/8. (0.625)

D3. A rope 13 m long is cut into 8 equal pieces. How long is each piece? Give
    the answer as a decimal.
              1.6 2 5
           ----------
        8 ) 1 3.0 0 0
            − 8
              -
              5 0
            − 4 8
              ---
                2 0
              − 1 6
                ---
                  4 0
                − 4 0
                  ---
                    0
    Answer: 1.625 m. 13/8 = 13 ÷ 8 = 1.625.
    Teacher explains: Sharing 13 among 8 is the division 13 ÷ 8, which is the
    fraction 13/8. Divide: 13 ÷ 8 = 1 R 5, write 1 and the point. Bring down a
    zero: 50 ÷ 8 = 6 R 2; 20 ÷ 8 = 2 R 4; 40 ÷ 8 = 5 R 0. Answer 1.625. Check
    with the mixed number: 13/8 = 1 5/8 and 5/8 = 0.625, so 1.625. Now you try:
    11/16. (0.6875)

D4. A bag of peanuts weighs 0.35 kg. Write this as a fraction of a kilogram in
    simplest form. Also write 0.125 and 2.75 as fractions.
    Answer: 0.35 = 35/100 = 7/20. 0.125 = 125/1000 = 1/8. 2.75 = 2 75/100 =
    2 3/4.
    Teacher explains: The last digit of 0.35 is in the hundredths place, so
    write it over 100: 35/100. Now simplify: the HCF of 35 and 100 is 5, so
    35/100 = 7/20. For 0.125 the last digit is in the thousandths place:
    125/1000, HCF 125, so 1/8. For 2.75 keep the 2 wholes and change 0.75 =
    75/100 = 3/4, giving 2 3/4. A common mistake is to write 0.35 = 35/10; count
    the decimal places: 2 places means 100. Now you try: 0.64 and 3.2. (16/25;
    3 1/5)

D5. Divide 1 by 3. What happens? How do we write the answer?
            0.3 3 3
           --------
        3 ) 1.0 0 0
            − 9
              -
              1 0
              − 9
                -
                1 0
                − 9
                  -
                  1
           ... remainder 1 again and again
    Answer: 1/3 = 0.333... for ever. We write 0.(3).
    Teacher explains: 1 ÷ 3 = 0 R 1, so write 0 and the point. Bring down a
    zero: 10 ÷ 3 = 3 R 1. Bring down another zero: 10 ÷ 3 = 3 R 1 again. The
    remainder is ALWAYS 1, so we will always get 10 ÷ 3 and the digit 3 for
    ever. This is a RECURRING (repeating) decimal. Writing the digits for ever
    is impossible, so we put the repeating block in brackets: 0.(3) means
    0.3333... Many books write 0.3 with a dot above the 3. Now you try: 2/3.
    (0.(6), because 20 ÷ 3 = 6 R 2 again and again)

D6. Write 1/6 as a decimal. Why is it 0.1(6) and not 0.(16)?
            0.1 6 6
           --------
        6 ) 1.0 0 0
            − 6
              -
              4 0
            − 3 6
              ---
                4 0
              − 3 6
                ---
                  4
            ... remainder 4 again
    Answer: 1/6 = 0.1666... = 0.1(6). Only the 6 repeats.
    Teacher explains: Divide: 10 ÷ 6 = 1 R 4. Then 40 ÷ 6 = 6 R 4. Then 40 ÷ 6 =
    6 R 4 again, for ever. The first digit 1 comes only once; after it the
    remainder 4 keeps coming back, so only the 6 repeats. The brackets go round
    the repeating block only: 0.1(6) = 0.16666..., but 0.(16) would mean
    0.161616..., a different number. Now you try: 5/6. (0.8(3) = 0.8333...)

D7. Find 1/7 as a decimal. How do you know when the digits start to repeat?
            0.1 4 2 8 5 7
           --------------
        7 ) 1.0 0 0 0 0 0
            − 7
              -
              3 0
            − 2 8
              ---
                2 0
              − 1 4
                ---
                  6 0
                − 5 6
                  ---
                    4 0
                  − 3 5
                    ---
                      5 0
                    − 4 9
                      ---
                        1
        remainders: 3, 2, 6, 4, 5, 1 → back to the start
    Answer: 1/7 = 0.142857142857... = 0.(142857).
    Teacher explains: Divide and watch the REMAINDERS: 10 ÷ 7 = 1 R 3, 30 ÷ 7 =
    4 R 2, 20 ÷ 7 = 2 R 6, 60 ÷ 7 = 8 R 4, 40 ÷ 7 = 5 R 5, 50 ÷ 7 = 7 R 1. The
    remainder 1 is where we started (1 ÷ 7), so everything repeats from here.
    When dividing by 7 the only possible remainders are 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 (a
    remainder of 0 would stop the decimal), so after at most 6 steps a remainder
    MUST repeat. That is why the block has 6 digits. Now you try: 2/7.
    (0.(285714): the same digits, starting at the 2)

D8. Find 1/9, 2/9, 1/11 and 2/11 as decimals. What patterns do you see?
    Answer: 1/9 = 0.(1), 2/9 = 0.(2), 1/11 = 0.(09), 2/11 = 0.(18).
    Teacher explains: For ninths: 10 ÷ 9 = 1 R 1 for ever, so 1/9 = 0.111... =
    0.(1). Then 2/9 = 2 × 0.111... = 0.222... = 0.(2), and in the same way 4/9 =
    0.(4) and 8/9 = 0.(8): the numerator repeats. For elevenths: 1 ÷ 11 = 0, 10
    ÷ 11 = 0 R 10, 100 ÷ 11 = 9 R 1, and then the remainder 1 returns, so 1/11 =
    0.090909... = 0.(09). Every eleventh has a 2-digit block that is 9 times the
    numerator: 2/11 = 0.(18), 3/11 = 0.(27), 5/11 = 0.(45). Now you try: 4/9 and
    3/11. (0.(4); 0.(27))

D9. Without dividing, say which of these give terminating decimals: 7/40, 5/12
    and 9/15.
    Answer: 7/40 terminates (0.175). 5/12 recurs (0.41(6)). 9/15 = 3/5
    terminates (0.6).
    Teacher explains: Step 1: write the fraction in simplest form. Step 2: break
    the denominator into primes. 40 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 5: only 2s and 5s, so 7/40
    terminates; indeed 7/40 = 175/1000 = 0.175. 12 = 2 × 2 × 3: there is a 3, so
    5/12 recurs: 5/12 = 0.41666... = 0.41(6). Careful with 9/15: 15 = 3 × 5 has
    a 3, but first simplify! 9/15 = 3/5, and 5 is fine, so it terminates: 0.6.
    The common mistake is to test the denominator before simplifying. Now you
    try: 7/35 and 3/14. (7/35 = 1/5 = 0.2 terminates; 14 = 2 × 7 so 3/14 recurs)

D10. Explain why a fraction whose denominator (in simplest form) has only 2s and
     5s as prime factors must terminate, and why 1/3 cannot.
     Answer: 10, 100, 1000, ... are made only of 2s and 5s (1000 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 5
     × 5 × 5), so such a denominator always divides some power of 10. No power
     of 10 is a multiple of 3, so 1/3 can never be written with denominator 10,
     100 or 1000.
     Teacher explains: A terminating decimal is a fraction over 10, 100, 1000,
     ... For 3/40 we need 40 to go into a power of 10: 40 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 5, and
     1000 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 5 × 5 × 5 contains all of those, so 3/40 = 75/1000 =
     0.075. But 1/3 would need 3 to go into 10, 100, 1000, ... and those numbers
     have no factor 3 (their digit sums are 1, so they are not multiples of 3).
     So 1/3 has no exact decimal, and the division goes on for ever. Now you
     try: can 1/12 be written over 1000? (No: 12 = 2 × 2 × 3 has a 3; 1/12 =
     0.08(3))

D11. Put these in order, smallest first: 3/5, 0.58, 5/8, 2/3, 0.65.
         3/5  = 0.600
         0.58 = 0.580
         5/8  = 0.625
         2/3  = 0.666...
         0.65 = 0.650
     Answer: 0.58, 3/5, 5/8, 0.65, 2/3.
     Teacher explains: Change everything into decimals with 3 places: 3/5 =
     0.600, 0.58 = 0.580, 5/8 = 0.625, 2/3 = 0.666..., 0.65 = 0.650. Now
     compare: 0.580 < 0.600 < 0.625 < 0.650 < 0.666... Then write the numbers
     back in their ORIGINAL form. For a recurring decimal, write enough digits
     to compare (0.666 is enough to see it is bigger than 0.650). Now you try:
     0.3, 1/3, 0.35, 2/7. (2/7 = 0.285..., so 2/7, 0.3, 1/3, 0.35)

D12. In a test Rahul scored 17 out of 20 and Sita scored 21 out of 25. Who did
     better? Then a calculator shows 5/6 as 0.8333333333: write it in bracket
     form and round it to 2 decimal places.
     Answer: Rahul: 17/20 = 0.85; Sita: 21/25 = 0.84. Rahul did better. 5/6 =
     0.8(3) ≈ 0.83.
     Teacher explains: The tests have different totals, so compare the fractions
     as decimals: 17/20 = 85/100 = 0.85 and 21/25 = 84/100 = 0.84. 0.85 > 0.84,
     so Rahul did (just) better, although Sita got more marks. A calculator can
     show only a few digits, so it cuts a recurring decimal short and rounds the
     last digit. 5/6 = 0.8333... = 0.8(3); to 2 places look at the third digit
     (3), which is less than 5, so 0.83. Now you try: 13/16 or 0.8, which is
     more? And round 2/3 to 2 places. (13/16 = 0.8125 is more; 0.67)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write as a decimal by making the denominator 10, 100 or 1000.
    a) 1/2    b) 1/4     c) 3/4     d) 1/5      e) 2/5    f) 3/5
    g) 4/5    h) 1/8     i) 3/8     j) 5/8      k) 7/8    l) 1/20
    m) 3/20   n) 1/25    o) 7/25    p) 1/40     q) 3/40   r) 1/50
    s) 9/50   t) 1/125   u) 7/200   v) 13/250
    Answers:
      a) 1/2 = 5/10 = 0.5
      b) 1/4 = 25/100 = 0.25
      c) 3/4 = 75/100 = 0.75
      d) 1/5 = 2/10 = 0.2
      e) 2/5 = 4/10 = 0.4
      f) 3/5 = 6/10 = 0.6
      g) 4/5 = 8/10 = 0.8
      h) 1/8 = 125/1000 = 0.125
      i) 3/8 = 375/1000 = 0.375
      j) 5/8 = 625/1000 = 0.625
      k) 7/8 = 875/1000 = 0.875
      l) 1/20 = 5/100 = 0.05
      m) 3/20 = 15/100 = 0.15
      n) 1/25 = 4/100 = 0.04
      o) 7/25 = 28/100 = 0.28
      p) 1/40 = 25/1000 = 0.025
      q) 3/40 = 75/1000 = 0.075
      r) 1/50 = 2/100 = 0.02
      s) 9/50 = 18/100 = 0.18
      t) 1/125 = 8/1000 = 0.008
      u) 7/200 = 35/1000 = 0.035
      v) 13/250 = 52/1000 = 0.052

M2. Write as a decimal. (Improper fractions and mixed numbers.)
    a) 5/4      b) 9/8      c) 2 3/5   d) 3 7/8     e) 11/20   f) 17/10
    g) 4 1/16   h) 6 3/40   i) 1 1/4   j) 25/8      k) 7/2     l) 13/5
    m) 21/20    n) 5 9/25   o) 33/25   p) 3 1/125   q) 19/4    r) 10 1/8
    Answers: a) 1.25   b) 1.125   c) 2.6   d) 3.875   e) 0.55   f) 1.7

             m) 1.05   n) 5.36   o) 1.32   p) 3.008   q) 4.75   r) 10.125

M3. Write as a fraction in simplest form.
    a) 0.5    b) 0.2     c) 0.25    d) 0.75    e) 0.4     f) 0.6
    g) 0.8    h) 0.125   i) 0.375   j) 0.625   k) 0.875   l) 0.05

             i) 375/1000 = 3/8   j) 625/1000 = 5/8   k) 875/1000 = 7/8

M4. Write as a mixed number in simplest form.
    a) 1.5     b) 2.25    c) 3.75    d) 4.2     e) 1.125   f) 5.05
    g) 10.4    h) 2.375   i) 6.08    j) 3.625   k) 7.8     l) 12.5

M5. Divide to write as a decimal. (These all terminate.)
    a) 1/16    b) 3/16    c) 7/32    d) 9/40    e) 11/80   f) 13/25

M6. Write as a recurring decimal using brackets (1/6 = 0.1(6)).
    a) 1/3    b) 2/3    c) 1/9    d) 2/9    e) 4/9    f) 5/9
    g) 7/9    h) 8/9    i) 1/6    j) 5/6    k) 1/11   l) 2/11
    m) 3/11   n) 5/11   o) 1/12   p) 5/12   q) 7/12   r) 1/15

M7. Harder recurring decimals. Divide until a remainder repeats.
    a) 1/7    b) 2/7     c) 3/7    d) 4/7     e) 5/7    f) 6/7
    g) 1/13   h) 1/14    i) 1/27   j) 1/33    k) 4/33   l) 1/37
    m) 1/99   n) 23/99   o) 1/21   p) 10/11
    Answers: a) 0.(142857)   b) 0.(285714)   c) 0.(428571)   d) 0.(571428)
             e) 0.(714285)   f) 0.(857142)   g) 0.(076923)   h) 0.0(714285)
             i) 0.(037)   j) 0.(03)   k) 0.(12)   l) 0.(027)   m) 0.(01)
             n) 0.(23)   o) 0.(047619)   p) 0.(90)

M8. Terminating (T) or recurring (R)? Simplify first, then break the denominator
    into primes. Then write the decimal.
    a) 3/8      b) 5/12    c) 7/20   d) 4/15    e) 9/16
    f) 2/9      g) 11/40   h) 6/15   i) 21/28   j) 7/30
    k) 13/125   l) 5/24    m) 9/36   n) 14/35   o) 3/22
    p) 17/50    q) 1/64    r) 5/48   s) 12/75   t) 7/56
    Answers:
      a) T: 8 = 2 × 2 × 2; 0.375
      b) R: 12 = 2 × 2 × 3; 0.41(6)
      c) T: 20 = 2 × 2 × 5; 0.35
      d) R: 15 = 3 × 5; 0.2(6)
      e) T: 16 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 2; 0.5625
      f) R: 9 = 3 × 3; 0.(2)
      g) T: 40 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 5; 0.275
      h) T: 6/15 = 2/5; 5 is prime; 0.4
      i) T: 21/28 = 3/4; 4 = 2 × 2; 0.75
      j) R: 30 = 2 × 3 × 5; 0.2(3)
      k) T: 125 = 5 × 5 × 5; 0.104
      l) R: 24 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 3; 0.208(3)
      m) T: 9/36 = 1/4; 4 = 2 × 2; 0.25
      n) T: 14/35 = 2/5; 5 is prime; 0.4
      o) R: 22 = 2 × 11; 0.1(36)
      p) T: 50 = 2 × 5 × 5; 0.34
      q) T: 64 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 2 × 2 × 2; 0.015625
      r) R: 48 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 2 × 3; 0.1041(6)
      s) T: 12/75 = 4/25; 25 = 5 × 5; 0.16
      t) T: 7/56 = 1/8; 8 = 2 × 2 × 2; 0.125

M9. Match each decimal with a fraction from the list.
    Choose from: 1/3, 2/3, 1/9, 4/9, 1/11, 2/11, 1/6, 5/6, 1/7, 3/11, 1/4, 8/9,

M11. Put in order, smallest first. Change each to a decimal first.

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: start with money (1/4 of a rupee = 25 paise = ₹0.25) and a 10 by
  10 grid for hundredths; keep a card of the key fractions and their decimals;
  do long division on squared paper and write each remainder in a column at the
  side so the child can SEE it repeat. Harder: find the length of the repeating
  block of 1/13, 1/17 and 1/19 and look for a link with the denominator; show
  that 0.(9) = 1 (1/3 = 0.(3), so 3 × 1/3 = 0.(9) = 1); change 0.(27) back to a
  fraction (27/99 = 3/11); investigate which fractions n/40, n/12 terminate.


================================================================================
TOPIC 5.10 — PERCENTAGES: MEANING, FRACTION/DECIMAL/PERCENT CONVERSION
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - PER CENT means "per hundred" or "out of 100". The sign % stands for /100:
    37% = 37/100. On a 10 by 10 grid, 37% is 37 of the 100 small squares.
  - The whole is 100%. If 37% is shaded, 100% − 37% = 63% is not shaded.
  - PERCENT → FRACTION: write over 100 and simplify: 35% = 35/100 = 7/20.
    PERCENT → DECIMAL: divide by 100 (move the point 2 places left): 35% = 0.35,
    4% = 0.04, 150% = 1.5.
  - FRACTION → PERCENT: make the denominator 100, or multiply by 100%: 3/4 =
    75/100 = 75%; 1/8 × 100% = 12.5%. DECIMAL → PERCENT: multiply by 100: 0.6 =
    60%, 1.25 = 125%, 0.075 = 7.5%.
  - Know the key set: 1% = 1/100 = 0.01, 5% = 1/20 = 0.05, 10% = 1/10 = 0.1,
    12.5% = 1/8 = 0.125, 20% = 1/5 = 0.2, 25% = 1/4 = 0.25, 33 1/3% = 1/3, 50% =
    1/2 = 0.5, 66 2/3% = 2/3, 75% = 3/4 = 0.75, 100% = 1, 150% = 1 1/2 = 1.5.
  - PERCENT OF A QUANTITY: "of" means multiply. 25% of 360 = 1/4 × 360 = 90. Or
    build it: 10% (÷ 10), 5% (half of 10%), 1% (÷ 100): 15% of 840 = 84 + 42 =
    126.
  - ONE QUANTITY AS A PERCENT OF ANOTHER: write part/whole as a fraction (same
    units first!) and change it to a percent: 45 cm of 3 m = 45/300 = 15%.
  - FINDING THE WHOLE (lightly): if 30% is 12, then 10% is 4 and 100% is 40.
  - A percent can be more than 100% (150% of 40 = 60), and percents make it easy
    to compare scores out of different totals.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A 10 by 10 grid has 37 small squares shaded. What percent is shaded? What
    percent is not shaded?
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
    Answer: 37% is shaded and 63% is not shaded.
    Teacher explains: The grid has 100 equal squares, so each square is 1 out of
    100, that is 1%. 37 squares out of 100 is 37/100, and "out of 100" is
    exactly what per cent means (from the Latin per centum, "for each hundred").
    The whole grid is 100/100 = 100%, so the unshaded part is 100% − 37% = 63%.
    Check: 37 + 63 = 100. Now you try: 8 squares shaded. (8% shaded, 92% not
    shaded)

D2. Priya scored 18 out of 25 in a maths test. What is her score as a percent?
    Answer: 72%. 18/25 = 72/100 = 72%.
    Teacher explains: A percent is a fraction out of 100, so make the
    denominator 100. 25 × 4 = 100, so multiply the top by 4 as well: 18 × 4 =
    72, giving 72/100 = 72%. Another way: 18/25 × 100% = 1800/25 % = 72%. When
    the total does not go into 100 easily, use the second way. Now you try: 45
    out of 50, and 21 out of 30. (90%; 70%)

D3. Write 35% as a fraction in simplest form and as a decimal. Then explain why
    4% is 0.04 and not 0.4.
    Answer: 35% = 35/100 = 7/20 = 0.35. 4% = 4/100 = 0.04.
    Teacher explains: Per cent means "out of 100", so 35% = 35/100. Simplify
    with the HCF 5: 7/20. As a decimal, 35 hundredths is 0.35: dividing by 100
    moves the digits 2 places to the right of the point. For 4%: 4/100 is 4
    HUNDREDTHS, so the 4 must be in the second place: 0.04. 0.4 would be 4
    tenths = 40/100 = 40%. Now you try: 8% and 80%. (8/100 = 2/25 = 0.08; 80/100
    = 4/5 = 0.8)

D4. Why is 1/8 equal to 12.5%, and 1/3 equal to 33 1/3%?
    Answer: 1/8 of 100 is 100 ÷ 8 = 12.5, so 1/8 = 12.5%. 1/3 of 100 is 100 ÷ 3
    = 33 1/3, so 1/3 = 33 1/3%.
    Teacher explains: To change a fraction to a percent, find that fraction of
    100%. 1/8 × 100% = 100/8 % = 12.5% (or 12 1/2%). For thirds, 100 ÷ 3 does
    not come out exactly: 3 × 33 = 99, and the 1 left over gives 1/3. So 1/3 =
    33 1/3% and 2/3 = 66 2/3%. Check: 33 1/3% + 66 2/3% = 100%. People often
    round 1/3 to 33%, but that is not exact. Learn the chain of eighths: 1/8 =
    12.5%, 3/8 = 37.5%, 5/8 = 62.5%, 7/8 = 87.5%. Now you try: 1/6. (100 ÷ 6 =
    16 2/3, so 16 2/3%)

D5. Write 0.6, 1.25 and 0.075 as percents.
    Answer: 0.6 = 60%, 1.25 = 125%, 0.075 = 7.5%.
    Teacher explains: To change a decimal to a percent, multiply by 100: the
    digits move 2 places to the left. 0.6 × 100 = 60, so 60%. A common mistake
    is 0.6 = 6%; but 0.6 = 6/10 = 60/100. 1.25 × 100 = 125: more than 1 whole
    means more than 100%. 0.075 × 100 = 7.5, so 7.5%. To go back, divide by 100:
    7.5% = 0.075. Now you try: 0.03 and 2.4. (3%; 240%)

D6. A school has 840 students. 15% of them come by bus. How many come by bus?
        100% → 840
         10% →  84
          5% →  42
         15% →  84 + 42 = 126
    Answer: 126 students. 10% of 840 = 84, 5% of 840 = 42, so 15% of 840 = 84 +
    42 = 126.
    Teacher explains: Build the percent from easy pieces. 10% is 1/10, so divide
    by 10: 840 ÷ 10 = 84. 5% is half of 10%: 84 ÷ 2 = 42. 15% = 10% + 5%, so 84
    + 42 = 126. Check with multiplication: 15/100 × 840 = 12600/100 = 126. Other
    pieces: 1% of 840 = 840 ÷ 100 = 8.4, 20% of 840 = 2 × 84 = 168, 50% of 840 =
    420. Now you try: 35% of 840. (3 × 84 + 42 = 294)

D7. A cycle costs ₹3,600. In a sale the price is cut by 25%. How much is taken
    off? What is 75% of ₹3,600?
    Answer: 25% of ₹3,600 = ₹900 is taken off. 75% of ₹3,600 = ₹2,700.
    Teacher explains: 25% is the same as 1/4, so find a quarter: 3,600 ÷ 4 =
    900. 75% is 3/4, three quarters: 3 × 900 = 2,700. Notice 25% + 75% = 100%
    and 900 + 2,700 = 3,600, so the sale price is ₹2,700. Using the fraction for
    a key percent is often the quickest way: 50% is half, 20% is a fifth, 12.5%
    is an eighth. Now you try: 75% of 64 and 20% of 45. (48; 9)

D8. Find 12% of 250. Then find 8% of 25 in your head.
    Answer: 12% of 250 = 30. 8% of 25 = 2.
    Teacher explains: "Of" means multiply: 12% of 250 = 12/100 × 250 = 3000/100
    = 30. Or with a decimal: 0.12 × 250 = 30. A useful trick: x% of y is always
    equal to y% of x, because both are x × y ÷ 100. So 8% of 25 = 25% of 8 = a
    quarter of 8 = 2. And 12% of 250: 250% of 12 = 2.5 × 12 = 30, the same. Now
    you try: 4% of 75 and 18% of 50. (75% of 4 = 3; 50% of 18 = 9)

D9. Meena cut 45 cm from a ribbon 3 m long. What percent of the ribbon did she
    cut?
    Answer: 15%. 45 cm out of 300 cm = 45/300 = 15/100 = 15%.
    Teacher explains: First put both lengths in the SAME unit: 3 m = 300 cm.
    Then write part over whole: 45/300. Simplify to make hundredths: divide top
    and bottom by 3: 15/100 = 15%. The common mistake is 45/3 = 1500%, which is
    clearly silly: she cannot cut more than the whole ribbon. Now you try: 250 g
    out of 2 kg, and 45 minutes out of 1 hour. (250/2000 = 12.5%; 45/60 = 75%)

D10. A plant was 40 cm tall. A month later it is 150% of its old height. How
     tall is it now? Can a percent be more than 100?
     Answer: 150% of 40 cm = 60 cm. Yes: more than 100% means more than the
     whole we started with.
     Teacher explains: 100% of 40 is the whole 40. 50% of 40 is 20. So 150% =
     100% + 50% gives 40 + 20 = 60 cm. As a decimal: 150% = 1.5, and 1.5 × 40 =
     60. A percent of a quantity can be more than 100% when something grows (a
     price rises, a plant grows), but a PART of a whole (the shaded part of a
     grid, marks in a test) can never be more than 100%. Now you try: 120% of 50
     and 200% of 35. (60; 70)

D11. In a class, 30% of the children are girls. There are 12 girls. How many
     children are in the class?
         |  4 |  4 |  4 |    |    |    |    |    |    |    |
          \____ 30% → 12 ____/
         10 parts of 4 → 40 → 100%
     Answer: 40 children. 30% → 12, 10% → 4, 100% → 40.
     Teacher explains: Here we know a part and want the whole. Go through an
     easy percent: 30% is 12, so 10% is 12 ÷ 3 = 4, and 100% is 4 × 10 = 40.
     Check: 30% of 40 = 3 × 4 = 12. A common mistake is to find 30% of 12 (3.6)
     instead: the 12 is already the 30%. Draw a bar for 100% cut into 10 equal
     parts; 3 parts are 12. Now you try: 25% of a sum is ₹45. What is the whole
     sum? (₹180)

D12. Asha scored 42 out of 60 in Hindi and 36 out of 48 in English. In which
     subject did she do better?
     Answer: English. Hindi: 42/60 = 70%. English: 36/48 = 75%.
     Teacher explains: The two tests have different totals, so comparing 42 and
     36 tells us nothing. Change both to percents. 42/60: divide by 6 to get
     7/10 = 70/100 = 70%. 36/48: divide by 12 to get 3/4 = 75%. 75% is more than
     70%, so English was better, even though she scored fewer marks. This is why
     schools and cricket statistics use percents: they make everything "out of
     100". Now you try: 17/20 or 21/25? (85% and 84%, so 17/20)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write as a percent.
    a) 37/100                  b) 5/100           c) 100/100
    d) 0/100                   e) 99/100          f) 1/100
    g) 50 out of 100           h) 7 per hundred   i) 64 hundredths
    j) 120/100                 k) 12.5/100        l) 3/10
    m) 9/10                    n) 1/10            o) shaded: 45 of 100
    p) not shaded: 45 of 100   q) 200/100         r) 1 whole
    Answers: a) 37%   b) 5%   c) 100%   d) 0%   e) 99%   f) 1%   g) 50%   h) 7%
             i) 64%   j) 120%   k) 12.5%   l) 30%   m) 90%   n) 10%   o) 45%
             p) 55%   q) 200%   r) 100%

M2. Write as a fraction in simplest form (use a mixed number or whole number
    when it is 100% or more).
    a) 1%    b) 2%    c) 5%     d) 8%        e) 10%       f) 12.5%
    g) 20%   h) 25%   i) 30%    j) 33 1/3%   k) 35%       l) 40%
    m) 45%   n) 50%   o) 60%    p) 64%       q) 66 2/3%   r) 75%
    s) 80%   t) 90%   u) 100%   v) 125%      w) 150%      x) 200%
    Answers: a) 1/100   b) 1/50   c) 1/20   d) 2/25   e) 1/10   f) 1/8   g) 1/5
             h) 1/4   i) 3/10   j) 1/3   k) 7/20   l) 2/5   m) 9/20   n) 1/2
             o) 3/5   p) 16/25   q) 2/3   r) 3/4   s) 4/5   t) 9/10   u) 1
             v) 1 1/4   w) 1 1/2   x) 2

M3. Write as a decimal.
    a) 1%      b) 7%     c) 10%    d) 12.5%   e) 25%
    f) 33%     g) 50%    h) 75%    i) 99%     j) 100%
    k) 150%    l) 250%   m) 0.5%   n) 4%      o) 40%
    p) 62.5%   q) 8.5%   r) 300%   s) 17%     t) 3.25%
    Answers: a) 0.01   b) 0.07   c) 0.1   d) 0.125   e) 0.25   f) 0.33   g) 0.5
             h) 0.75   i) 0.99   j) 1   k) 1.5   l) 2.5   m) 0.005   n) 0.04
             o) 0.4   p) 0.625   q) 0.085   r) 3   s) 0.17   t) 0.0325

M4. Write as a percent.
    a) 0.5     b) 0.25   c) 0.07   d) 0.7     e) 0.125
    f) 1       g) 1.5    h) 0.01   i) 0.045   j) 2
    k) 0.9     l) 0.36   m) 0.08   n) 1.75    o) 0.005
    p) 0.625   q) 3.2    r) 0.99   s) 0.4     t) 0.333
    Answers: a) 50%   b) 25%   c) 7%   d) 70%   e) 12.5%   f) 100%   g) 150%
             h) 1%   i) 4.5%   j) 200%   k) 90%   l) 36%   m) 8%   n) 175%
             o) 0.5%   p) 62.5%   q) 320%   r) 99%   s) 40%   t) 33.3%

M5. Write as a percent.
    a) 1/2    b) 1/4    c) 3/4    d) 1/5    e) 2/5     f) 3/5
    g) 4/5    h) 1/10   i) 3/10   j) 7/10   k) 1/8     l) 3/8
    m) 5/8    n) 7/8    o) 1/3    p) 2/3    q) 1/6     r) 1/20

    Answers: a) 50%   b) 25%   c) 75%   d) 20%   e) 40%   f) 60%   g) 80%
             h) 10%   i) 30%   j) 70%   k) 12.5%   l) 37.5%   m) 62.5%
             n) 87.5%   o) 33 1/3%   p) 66 2/3%   q) 16 2/3%   r) 5%   s) 4%
             t) 2%   u) 150%   v) 125%   w) 55%   x) 28%

M6. Copy and complete the table.
       Fraction | Decimal | Percent
       ---------+---------+---------

M7. Find. (Use 10% = ÷ 10, 50% = half, 25% = quarter, 1% = ÷ 100.)
    a) 10% of 70    b) 10% of 350   c) 10% of 45   d) 50% of 64
    e) 50% of 90    f) 50% of 7     g) 25% of 80   h) 25% of 360
    i) 25% of 50    j) 1% of 300    k) 1% of 450   l) 1% of 25
    m) 5% of 60     n) 5% of 240    o) 20% of 45   p) 20% of 150
    q) 100% of 38   r) 0% of 500    s) 75% of 64   t) 10% of 5
    Answers: a) 7   b) 35   c) 4.5   d) 32   e) 45   f) 3.5   g) 20   h) 90
             i) 12.5   j) 3   k) 4.5   l) 0.25   m) 3   n) 12   o) 9   p) 30
             q) 38   r) 0   s) 48   t) 0.5

M8. Find.
    a) 15% of 840     b) 35% of 840   c) 12.5% of 64     d) 33 1/3% of 90
    e) 75% of 3,600   f) 150% of 40   g) 2.5% of 200     h) 60% of 55
    i) 12% of 250     j) 8% of 25     k) 45% of 120      l) 66 2/3% of 45

    Answers: a) 126   b) 294   c) 8   d) 30   e) 2,700   f) 60   g) 5   h) 33
             i) 30   j) 2   k) 54   l) 30   m) 60   n) 51   o) 18   p) 3   q) 27

M9. Find the whole. (Example: 30% of __ = 12, so 10% is 4 and 100% is 40.)
    a) 30% of __ = 12       b) 10% of __ = 7    c) 25% of __ = 45
    d) 50% of __ = 36       e) 20% of __ = 9    f) 1% of __ = 3

             i) 45   j) 40   k) 70   l) 60   m) 33   n) 50

M10. What percent is the first of the second? (Same units first.)
     a) 3 of 4          b) 12 of 50          c) 45 of 60
     d) 18 of 24        e) 7 of 20           f) 9 of 12

              h) 25%   i) 90%   j) 33 1/3%   k) 25%   l) 25%   m) 75%   n) 25%
              o) 20%   p) 2%   q) 12.5%   r) 120%   s) 25%   t) 30%

M11. Write <, > or =.
     a) 40% __ 3/8    b) 0.45 __ 44%   c) 1/3 __ 33%     d) 2/5 __ 45%
     e) 0.7 __ 70%    f) 1/8 __ 12%    g) 3/4 __ 0.8     h) 66% __ 2/3
     i) 1.2 __ 112%   j) 5% __ 0.5     k) 9/20 __ 0.45   l) 1/6 __ 17%
     m) 0.09 __ 9%    n) 7/8 __ 88%    o) 150% __ 3/2    p) 0.3 __ 33 1/3%
     Answers: a) >   b) >   c) >   d) <   e) =   f) >   g) <   h) <   i) >
              j) <   k) =   l) <   m) =   n) <   o) =   p) <

M12. The whole is 100%. If this part is shaded, what percent is NOT shaded?
     a) 37%    b) 8%      c) 63%       d) 50%   e) 99%   f) 12.5%   g) 0%
     h) 100%   i) 45.5%   j) 33 1/3%   k) 72%   l) 1%    m) 87.5%   n) 25%
     o) 60%    p) 5%
     Answers: a) 63%   b) 92%   c) 37%   d) 50%   e) 1%   f) 87.5%   g) 100%
              h) 0%   i) 54.5%   j) 66 2/3%   k) 28%   l) 99%   m) 12.5%
              n) 75%   o) 40%   p) 95%

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: shade 10 by 10 grids and count squares; use money (₹1 = 100 paise,
  so 25 paise is 25% of a rupee); make a key-percent card (1%, 10%, 25%, 50%,
  75%) with fraction and decimal side by side; for percent of a quantity always
  start from 10% and 1%. Harder: percent of a percent (10% of 50%), finding the
  whole from any percent (35% is 21, find 100%), comparing cricket strike rates
  and test scores as percents, and why a 50% rise followed by a 50% fall does
  not bring you back to the start (preview of 5.30).


================================================================================
TOPIC 5.11 — RATIO AND RATE; EQUIVALENT RATIOS; UNIT RATE
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - A RATIO compares two quantities of the same kind by division. 12 boys and 18
    girls: the ratio of boys to girls is 12 : 18, read "12 to 18". It can also
    be written as the fraction 12/18.
  - ORDER MATTERS: boys : girls = 12 : 18 but girls : boys = 18 : 12.
  - SIMPLEST FORM: divide both terms by their HCF: 12 : 18 = 2 : 3 (HCF 6). A
    ratio in simplest form has whole-number terms with no common factor except
    1.
  - SAME UNITS FIRST: 50 cm : 2 m = 50 cm : 200 cm = 1 : 4. A ratio of two
    quantities of the same kind has no units.
  - Ratios with decimals or fractions: multiply both terms to make whole
    numbers: 1.5 : 2.5 = 15 : 25 = 3 : 5; 1/2 : 1/3 = 3 : 2 (× 6).
  - EQUIVALENT RATIOS: multiply or divide BOTH terms by the same non-zero
    number: 2 : 3 = 4 : 6 = 6 : 9 = 20 : 30. Adding the same number does NOT
    give an equivalent ratio (2 : 3 is not 3 : 4).
  - MISSING TERM: 3 : 5 = 12 : __. 3 × 4 = 12, so 5 × 4 = 20.
  - COMPARING RATIOS: write them as fractions and compare (common denominator or
    decimals): 2 : 5 = 0.4 > 3 : 8 = 0.375.
  - Part to part and part to whole: if boys : girls = 2 : 3, then boys are 2/5
    of the class (2 + 3 = 5 parts in all).
  - A RATE compares two quantities of DIFFERENT kinds, so it keeps its units: 90
    km/h, ₹48 per kg, 45 words per minute. A UNIT RATE is the amount for ONE
    unit. The BEST BUY has the lowest price per unit.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. In a class there are 12 boys and 18 girls. Write the ratio of boys to girls,
    and of girls to boys, in simplest form.
        B B | B B | B B | B B | B B | B B     6 groups
        G G G | G G G | G G G | G G G | G G G | G G G
    Answer: boys : girls = 12 : 18 = 2 : 3. girls : boys = 18 : 12 = 3 : 2.
    Teacher explains: A ratio compares two amounts. "Boys to girls" means the
    boys' number comes first: 12 : 18. Both numbers can be divided by 6 (their
    HCF), giving 2 : 3. This means: for every 2 boys there are 3 girls. Picture
    it: make groups of 2 boys and 3 girls: you get exactly 6 such groups. The
    order of the words gives the order of the numbers, so girls to boys is
    3 : 2, a different ratio. Now you try: 15 mangoes and 25 apples; mangoes to
    apples. (3 : 5)

D2. Write 36 : 48 in simplest form. Show two ways.
    Answer: 36 : 48 = 3 : 4.
    Teacher explains: Way 1: divide by the HCF in one step. Factors of 36 and
    48: the HCF is 12. 36 ÷ 12 = 3 and 48 ÷ 12 = 4, so 3 : 4. Way 2: divide by
    small common factors again and again: 36 : 48 → (÷ 2) 18 : 24 → (÷ 2) 9 : 12
    → (÷ 3) 3 : 4. Stop when the only common factor is 1. Check: 3 × 12 = 36 and
    4 × 12 = 48. A common mistake is to stop too early at 9 : 12. Now you try:
    45 : 60 and 84 : 56. (3 : 4; 3 : 2)

D3. A stick is 50 cm long and a rod is 2 m long. Find the ratio of their
    lengths. Also find the ratio 45 minutes : 2 hours.
    Answer: 50 cm : 2 m = 50 : 200 = 1 : 4. 45 min : 2 h = 45 : 120 = 3 : 8.
    Teacher explains: Before writing a ratio, change both quantities into the
    SAME unit. 2 m = 200 cm, so the ratio is 50 cm : 200 cm; now the units are
    the same, so drop them: 50 : 200 = 1 : 4 (÷ 50). The rod is 4 times as long.
    The common mistake is 50 : 2 = 25 : 1, which says the stick is 25 times
    LONGER, which is silly. In the same way 2 h = 120 min, so 45 : 120 = 3 : 8
    (÷ 15). Now you try: 600 g : 1.5 kg. (600 : 1500 = 2 : 5)

D4. Amma cooks rice with 2 cups of rice to 3 cups of water. For a party she uses
    6 cups of rice. How much water? Is 3 : 4 equivalent to 2 : 3?
    Answer: 9 cups of water (2 : 3 = 6 : 9). No, 3 : 4 is not equivalent to
    2 : 3.
    Teacher explains: To keep the rice tasting the same, the ratio must stay the
    same. 6 cups is 3 times 2 cups, so the water must also be 3 times: 3 × 3 =
    9. Equivalent ratios come from MULTIPLYING (or dividing) both terms by the
    same number: 2 : 3 = 4 : 6 = 6 : 9 = 8 : 12. Adding 1 to each term (2 : 3 →
    3 : 4) changes the ratio: 2/3 is about 0.67 but 3/4 = 0.75, so the rice
    would be too dry. Now you try: is 5 : 8 = 15 : 24? Is 4 : 5 = 6 : 7? (yes, ×
    3; no)

D5. Find the missing numbers: 3 : 5 = 12 : __ and 3 : 5 = __ : 35.
    Answer: 3 : 5 = 12 : 20 and 3 : 5 = 21 : 35.
    Teacher explains: Look for the multiplier. From 3 to 12 we multiply by 4, so
    multiply 5 by 4 as well: 20. From 5 to 35 we multiply by 7, so 3 × 7 = 21.
    Always ask "what was the first term multiplied by?" and do the same to the
    other term. You can check with fractions: 3/5 = 12/20 = 21/35. Sometimes you
    must divide: 24 : 36 = 2 : __ (÷ 12, so 3). Now you try: 4 : 7 = __ : 56.
    (32)

D6. In a bag the ratio of red beads to blue beads is 2 : 3. What fraction of the
    beads are red? Ravi says 2/3. Is he right?
        R R B B B | R R B B B | ...   each group: 2 red of 5 beads
    Answer: No. Red beads are 2/5 of all the beads (and blue are 3/5).
    Teacher explains: The ratio 2 : 3 compares a part with a part: for every 2
    red beads there are 3 blue beads. So in each group of 2 + 3 = 5 beads, 2 are
    red. The fraction of ALL beads that are red is 2/5. The fraction 2/3
    compares red with blue (red is 2/3 of the blue ones), which is a different
    question. Always add the parts to get the whole. Now you try: red : blue =
    3 : 4; what fraction is red? What fraction is blue? (3/7; 4/7)

D7. Lemonade A is made with 2 glasses of lemon juice to 5 glasses of water.
    Lemonade B uses 3 glasses of juice to 8 glasses of water. Which tastes more
    lemony?
    Answer: A. 2 : 5 = 2/5 = 16/40 and 3 : 8 = 3/8 = 15/40, so A has more juice
    for each glass of water.
    Teacher explains: To compare ratios, write each as a fraction (juice/water)
    and compare. Use a common denominator: 2/5 = 16/40 and 3/8 = 15/40. 16/40 >
    15/40, so A has more juice per glass of water. Or use decimals: 2/5 = 0.4
    and 3/8 = 0.375. The mistake is to say "B has more juice (3 glasses)": B
    also has more water. Now you try: 4 : 9 or 3 : 7? (4/9 = 28/63 > 3/7 =
    27/63, so 4 : 9)

D8. Write in simplest form: 1.5 : 2.5, and 1/2 : 1/3.
    Answer: 1.5 : 2.5 = 15 : 25 = 3 : 5. 1/2 : 1/3 = 3 : 2.
    Teacher explains: A simplest-form ratio has whole numbers. For decimals,
    multiply both terms by 10 (or 100) to remove the point: 1.5 : 2.5 = 15 : 25,
    then divide by 5: 3 : 5. For fractions, multiply both by a common
    denominator (the LCM of 2 and 3 is 6): 1/2 × 6 = 3 and 1/3 × 6 = 2, so
    3 : 2. Check: 1/2 is bigger than 1/3, and 3 is bigger than 2. Now you try:
    0.4 : 1.2 and 2/3 : 3/4. (1 : 3; 8 : 9)

D9. A train travels 360 km in 4 hours at a steady speed. What is its speed? How
    is a rate different from a ratio?
    Answer: 90 km per hour (90 km/h). 360 ÷ 4 = 90.
    Teacher explains: A rate compares two DIFFERENT kinds of quantity, here
    kilometres and hours, so the units stay in the answer: km per hour, written
    km/h. The UNIT RATE is the amount for 1 hour: 360 km ÷ 4 h = 90 km in 1
    hour. A ratio like 12 : 18 compares the same kind of thing and has no units.
    Other rates: ₹ per kg, words per minute, runs per over, heartbeats per
    minute. Now you try: 225 km in 3 hours. (75 km/h)

D10. 5 kg of rice costs ₹240. What is the price of 1 kg? What do 8 kg cost?
     Answer: ₹48 per kg. 8 kg cost ₹384.
     Teacher explains: First find the unit rate, the price for ONE kilogram:
     ₹240 ÷ 5 = ₹48 per kg. Then 8 kg cost 8 × ₹48 = ₹384. Check it is sensible:
     8 kg is a bit less than twice 5 kg, and ₹384 is a bit less than twice ₹240.
     Shopkeepers write unit rates on labels, like "₹48/kg". (More of this in
     5.13, the unitary method.) Now you try: 6 pens cost ₹72. What is the price
     of one pen, and of 10 pens? (₹12; ₹120)

D11. Toothpaste A: 150 g for ₹90. Toothpaste B: 200 g for ₹116. Which is the
     better buy?
     Answer: B. A costs ₹0.60 per gram (₹60 per 100 g); B costs ₹0.58 per gram
     (₹58 per 100 g).
     Teacher explains: The bigger tube costs more, so compare the price for the
     SAME amount. Unit price of A: 90 ÷ 150 = 0.6, so ₹0.60 per gram. Unit price
     of B: 116 ÷ 200 = 0.58, so ₹0.58 per gram. B is cheaper per gram, so B is
     the better buy (if you will use it all). Comparing per 100 g avoids small
     decimals: A is ₹90 for 150 g, so ₹30 per 50 g and ₹60 per 100 g; B is ₹58
     per 100 g. Now you try: 500 mL for ₹45 or 1 L for ₹84? (₹9 or ₹8.40 per 100
     mL, so the 1 L bottle)

D12. Meena typed 540 words in 12 minutes. Ravi typed 400 words in 8 minutes. Who
     types faster? How long does Ravi take for one word?
     Answer: Ravi. Meena: 540 ÷ 12 = 45 words per minute. Ravi: 400 ÷ 8 = 50
     words per minute. Ravi takes 60 ÷ 50 = 1.2 seconds per word.
     Teacher explains: They typed for different times, so compare unit rates:
     words in ONE minute. 45 < 50, so Ravi is faster. A rate can be turned
     upside down: 50 words per minute means 1 minute (60 seconds) for 50 words,
     so 60 ÷ 50 = 1.2 seconds per word. For speed the bigger "per minute" number
     is faster, but the bigger "seconds per word" number is slower. Now you try:
     a heart beats 144 times in 2 minutes. What is the rate per minute? (72
     beats per minute)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write in simplest form.
    a) 12 : 18   b) 4 : 6     c) 10 : 15   d) 8 : 12    e) 6 : 9
    f) 14 : 21   g) 5 : 20    h) 9 : 3     i) 16 : 24   j) 20 : 30
    k) 7 : 28    l) 18 : 12   m) 15 : 25   n) 21 : 14   o) 30 : 45
    p) 24 : 8    q) 11 : 33   r) 27 : 36   s) 40 : 60   t) 6 : 6
    Answers: a) 2 : 3   b) 2 : 3   c) 2 : 3   d) 2 : 3   e) 2 : 3   f) 2 : 3
             g) 1 : 4   h) 3 : 1   i) 2 : 3   j) 2 : 3   k) 1 : 4   l) 3 : 2
             m) 3 : 5   n) 3 : 2   o) 2 : 3   p) 3 : 1   q) 1 : 3   r) 3 : 4
             s) 2 : 3   t) 1 : 1

M2. Write in simplest form. (Find the HCF first.)

    m) 132 : 88    n) 75 : 105    o) 360 : 240     p) 57 : 76
    q) 140 : 196   r) 600 : 450   s) 221 : 247     t) 1,000 : 125
    Answers: a) 3 : 4   b) 3 : 4   c) 3 : 2   d) 3 : 4   e) 5 : 3   f) 4 : 3
             g) 5 : 7   h) 4 : 5   i) 7 : 3   j) 4 : 7   k) 1 : 4   l) 3 : 2
             m) 3 : 2   n) 5 : 7   o) 3 : 2   p) 3 : 4   q) 5 : 7   r) 4 : 3
             s) 17 : 19   t) 8 : 1

M3. Change to the same unit, then write in simplest form.
    a) 50 cm : 2 m       b) 300 g : 1 kg       c) 45 min : 1 h
    d) 2 km : 500 m      e) 750 mL : 2 L       f) 1 h 30 min : 45 min
    g) ₹2 : 50 paise     h) 3 days : 2 weeks   i) 8 months : 2 years
    j) 1.2 m : 80 cm     k) 600 g : 1.5 kg     l) 45 min : 2 h
    m) 40 s : 2 min      n) 25 paise : ₹1      o) 1 km : 250 m
    p) 500 mL : 1.25 L   q) 9 mm : 3 cm        r) 2 dozen : 30
    Answers: a) 1 : 4   b) 3 : 10   c) 3 : 4   d) 4 : 1   e) 3 : 8   f) 2 : 1
             g) 4 : 1   h) 3 : 14   i) 1 : 3   j) 3 : 2   k) 2 : 5   l) 3 : 8
             m) 1 : 3   n) 1 : 4   o) 4 : 1   p) 2 : 5   q) 3 : 10   r) 4 : 5

M4. Write in simplest form with whole numbers.
    a) 1.5 : 2.5    b) 1.5 : 2     c) 0.2 : 0.8       d) 2.5 : 7.5
    e) 0.6 : 0.45   f) 0.4 : 1.2   g) 3.2 : 0.8       h) 1.25 : 0.5
    i) 1/2 : 1/4    j) 2/3 : 1/2   k) 3/4 : 5/6       l) 1/2 : 1/3
    m) 2/3 : 3/4    n) 1 1/2 : 2   o) 2 1/2 : 1 1/4   p) 1/5 : 0.3
    Answers: a) 3 : 5   b) 3 : 4   c) 1 : 4   d) 1 : 3   e) 4 : 3   f) 1 : 3
             g) 4 : 1   h) 5 : 2   i) 2 : 1   j) 4 : 3   k) 9 : 10   l) 3 : 2
             m) 8 : 9   n) 3 : 4   o) 2 : 1   p) 2 : 3

M5. Write the next three equivalent ratios (× 2, × 3, × 4).
    a) 1 : 2    b) 2 : 3   c) 3 : 5    d) 4 : 7   e) 5 : 6
    f) 7 : 10   g) 9 : 4   h) 1 : 12   i) 3 : 8   j) 11 : 5
    Answers: a) 2 : 4, 3 : 6, 4 : 8   b) 4 : 6, 6 : 9, 8 : 12
             c) 6 : 10, 9 : 15, 12 : 20   d) 8 : 14, 12 : 21, 16 : 28
             e) 10 : 12, 15 : 18, 20 : 24   f) 14 : 20, 21 : 30, 28 : 40
             g) 18 : 8, 27 : 12, 36 : 16   h) 2 : 24, 3 : 36, 4 : 48
             i) 6 : 16, 9 : 24, 12 : 32   j) 22 : 10, 33 : 15, 44 : 20

M6. Find the missing term.
    a) 3 : 5 = 12 : __     b) 3 : 5 = __ : 35    c) 4 : 7 = __ : 56
    d) 2 : 9 = 10 : __     e) 5 : 8 = __ : 48    f) 1 : 6 = 7 : __
    g) 24 : 36 = 2 : __    h) 45 : 30 = __ : 2   i) 7 : 4 = 63 : __
    j) 6 : 11 = __ : 99    k) 12 : 16 = __ : 4   l) 9 : 15 = 3 : __
    m) 2 : 5 = __ : 1000   n) 10 : 3 = 70 : __   o) 8 : 5 = __ : 45
    p) 35 : 21 = 5 : __    q) 1 : 4 = 25 : __    r) 5 : 9 = __ : 72
    s) 60 : 80 = __ : 4    t) 13 : 7 = 39 : __
    Answers: a) 20   b) 21   c) 32   d) 45   e) 30   f) 42   g) 3   h) 3   i) 36
             j) 54   k) 3   l) 5   m) 400   n) 21   o) 72   p) 3   q) 100
             r) 40   s) 3   t) 21

M7. Equivalent? Write yes or no.
    a) 2 : 3 and 4 : 6       b) 2 : 3 and 3 : 4     c) 5 : 8 and 15 : 24
    d) 4 : 5 and 6 : 7       e) 3 : 7 and 9 : 21    f) 1 : 4 and 4 : 1
    g) 6 : 9 and 8 : 12      h) 10 : 4 and 15 : 6   i) 3 : 5 and 5 : 7
    j) 12 : 18 and 18 : 27   k) 7 : 2 and 21 : 5    l) 9 : 12 and 12 : 16
    m) 1 : 3 and 11 : 33     n) 5 : 6 and 6 : 5     o) 0.5 : 2 and 2 : 8
    p) 16 : 20 and 20 : 24
    Answers: a) yes   b) no   c) yes   d) no   e) yes   f) no   g) yes   h) yes
             i) no   j) yes   k) no   l) yes   m) yes   n) no   o) yes   p) no

M8. Compare the ratios (first term ÷ second term). Write <, > or =.
    a) 2 : 5 __ 3 : 8      b) 4 : 9 __ 3 : 7     c) 1 : 2 __ 3 : 5
    d) 5 : 6 __ 7 : 9      e) 3 : 4 __ 9 : 12    f) 2 : 7 __ 1 : 3
    g) 7 : 10 __ 2 : 3     h) 5 : 12 __ 3 : 8    i) 4 : 3 __ 5 : 4
    j) 9 : 20 __ 11 : 25   k) 1 : 6 __ 2 : 11    l) 3 : 10 __ 6 : 20
    m) 8 : 15 __ 5 : 9     n) 13 : 20 __ 2 : 3   o) 7 : 8 __ 8 : 9
    p) 11 : 4 __ 8 : 3
    Answers: a) >   b) >   c) <   d) >   e) =   f) <   g) >   h) >   i) >   j) >
             k) <   l) =   m) <   n) <   o) <   p) >

M9. The ratio is first : second. What fraction of the whole is the first part?
    The second part? (Answers: first; second.)
    a) 2 : 3   b) 3 : 4   c) 1 : 1   d) 5 : 3    e) 1 : 4    f) 7 : 2
    g) 3 : 7   h) 4 : 5   i) 2 : 9   j) 5 : 7    k) 11 : 1   l) 3 : 5
    m) 6 : 5   n) 1 : 9   o) 8 : 3   p) 7 : 13
    Answers: a) 2/5; 3/5   b) 3/7; 4/7   c) 1/2; 1/2   d) 5/8; 3/8   e) 1/5; 4/5
             f) 7/9; 2/9   g) 3/10; 7/10   h) 4/9; 5/9   i) 2/11; 9/11
             j) 5/12; 7/12   k) 11/12; 1/12   l) 3/8; 5/8   m) 6/11; 5/11
             n) 1/10; 9/10   o) 8/11; 3/11   p) 7/20; 13/20

M10. Find the unit rate.
     a) 360 km in 4 h         b) ₹240 for 5 kg        c) 540 words in 12 min
     d) 96 pages in 8 days    e) 1,200 L in 15 min    f) ₹84 for 12 eggs
     g) 45 km on 3 L          h) 144 beats in 2 min   i) ₹1,500 for 6 days
     j) 72 runs in 12 overs   k) ₹45 for 3 pens       l) 250 km in 5 h
     m) ₹63 for 1.5 kg        n) 18 km in 1.5 h       o) ₹7.50 for 5 pencils
     p) 3.6 L in 12 min       q) ₹1,000 for 8 kg      r) 600 m in 4 min
     s) ₹90 for 150 g         t) 5 km in 20 min
     Answers: a) 90 km/h   b) ₹48 per kg   c) 45 words per min
              d) 12 pages per day   e) 80 L per min   f) ₹7 per egg
              g) 15 km per L   h) 72 beats per min   i) ₹250 per day
              j) 6 runs per over   k) ₹15 per pen   l) 50 km/h   m) ₹42 per kg
              n) 12 km/h   o) ₹1.50 per pencil   p) 0.3 L per min
              q) ₹125 per kg   r) 150 m per min   s) ₹0.60 per g
              t) 0.25 km per min

M11. Use the unit rate.
     a) ₹48 per kg: cost of 3.5 kg       b) 90 km/h: distance in 3 h
     c) 45 words/min: words in 20 min    d) ₹12 per pen: pens for ₹96
     e) 15 km per L: litres for 240 km   f) ₹0.58 per g: cost of 200 g
     g) 72 beats/min: beats in 5 min     h) 60 km/h: time for 150 km
     i) ₹250 per day: days for ₹1,750    j) ₹6.50 per egg: cost of 12 eggs
     k) 6 runs/over: runs in 20 overs    l) 80 L per min: time for 1,000 L
     m) ₹35 per m: cost of 2.4 m         n) 50 m per min: distance in 1 h
     o) ₹16 per 100 g: cost of 1 kg      p) 12 pages/day: days for 180 pages
     Answers: a) ₹168   b) 270 km   c) 900 words   d) 8 pens   e) 16 L   f) ₹116
              g) 360 beats   h) 2.5 h   i) 7 days   j) ₹78   k) 120 runs
              l) 12.5 min   m) ₹84   n) 3,000 m   o) ₹160   p) 15 days

M12. Which is the better buy? Find the price per unit (per 100 g or 100 mL for
     grams and millilitres).
     a) A: 150 g ₹90  B: 200 g ₹116      b) A: 500 mL ₹45  B: 1,000 mL ₹84
     c) A: 250 g ₹60  B: 400 g ₹92       d) A: 2 kg ₹150  B: 5 kg ₹360
     e) A: 6 eggs ₹54  B: 10 eggs ₹85    f) A: 200 g ₹50  B: 500 g ₹130
     g) A: 1 L ₹55  B: 3 L ₹159          h) A: 12 pens ₹90  B: 20 pens ₹148
     i) A: 750 g ₹120  B: 1,000 g ₹164   j) A: 4 soaps ₹100  B: 6 soaps ₹147
     k) A: 300 mL ₹75  B: 450 mL ₹108    l) A: 5 kg ₹240  B: 8 kg ₹392
     Answers: a) A ₹60, B ₹58 per 100 g; B   b) A ₹9, B ₹8.40 per 100 mL; B
              c) A ₹24, B ₹23 per 100 g; B   d) A ₹75, B ₹72 per kg; B
              e) A ₹9, B ₹8.50 per egg; B   f) A ₹25, B ₹26 per 100 g; A
              g) A ₹55, B ₹53 per L; B   h) A ₹7.50, B ₹7.40 per pen; B
              i) A ₹16, B ₹16.40 per 100 g; A   j) A ₹25, B ₹24.50 per soap; B
              k) A ₹25, B ₹24 per 100 mL; B   l) A ₹48, B ₹49 per kg; A

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: use real counters in two colours and make groups; say ratios aloud
  as "for every 2 boys there are 3 girls"; always write the words above the
  numbers (boys : girls) so the order is right; for rates, write the unit in
  every line of working. Harder: ratios with three terms (2 : 3 : 4, see 5.12),
  comparing cricket strike rates and economy rates, working out which pack of
  biscuits is the best buy on a real shop visit, and rates that are turned
  upside down (km per L and L per km).


================================================================================
TOPIC 5.12 — RATIO TABLES AND DOUBLE NUMBER LINES; SHARING IN A GIVEN RATIO
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - A RATIO TABLE lists equivalent ratios in columns. Move from column to column
    by MULTIPLYING or DIVIDING both rows by the same number, or by ADDING two
    columns (2 : 3 and 4 : 6 add to 6 : 9).
  - A good route: first go DOWN to 1 (divide), then UP to any amount (multiply).
    For 6 eggs → 250 g, 3 eggs → 125 g, 15 eggs → 625 g.
  - A DOUBLE NUMBER LINE is two number lines with the same marks: each mark
    pairs two quantities in the same ratio (3 km ↔ 40 min, 6 km ↔ 80 min).
    Halfway between two marks is halfway on both lines.
  - SHARING IN A RATIO a : b: add the parts (a + b), find ONE part (total ÷
    number of parts), then multiply: ₹450 in 2 : 3 → 5 parts, 1 part = ₹90,
    shares ₹180 and ₹270. Check that the shares add up to the total.
  - Draw a BAR MODEL: one bar cut into equal boxes, a boxes for the first person
    and b boxes for the second.
  - If ONE SHARE is known: shares 5 : 4 and the first is 35, so 1 part = 7, the
    second is 28 and the total is 63.
  - If the DIFFERENCE is known: 7 : 3 and the difference is 120, so 4 parts =
    120 and 1 part = 30.
  - THREE-PART RATIOS a : b : c work the same way: ₹1,800 in 2 : 3 : 4 → 9
    parts, 1 part = ₹200, shares ₹400, ₹600, ₹800.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A cake recipe uses 3 cups of flour for every 2 cups of milk. Complete the
    ratio table. How much milk goes with 1.5 cups of flour?
        flour |  1.5 |     3 |     6 |     9 |    12 |    15 |
        milk  |   __ |     2 |    __ |    __ |    __ |    __ |
    Answer: Milk: 1 cup (for 1.5), 4 (for 6), 6 (for 9), 8 (for 12), 10 (for
    15).
    Teacher explains: Each column is an equivalent ratio. From 3 to 6 is × 2, so
    milk 2 × 2 = 4. From 3 to 9 is × 3, so 6 cups. For 12, either × 4 (2 × 4 =
    8) or ADD the columns for 3 and 9: 2 + 6 = 8. For 15, add the columns for 6
    and 9: 4 + 6 = 10. For 1.5, halve the first column: 3 ÷ 2 = 1.5, 2 ÷ 2 = 1.
    Whatever you do to the flour, do to the milk. Now you try: flour 4.5 and 30.
    (milk 3 and 20)

D2. 4 notebooks cost ₹60. Use a ratio table to find the cost of 1, 7 and 10
    notebooks.
        books |    4 |     1 |     7 |    10 |
        ₹     |   60 |    __ |    __ |    __ |
    Answer: 1 notebook ₹15, 7 notebooks ₹105, 10 notebooks ₹150.
    Teacher explains: Go DOWN to 1 first: divide both rows by 4: 4 ÷ 4 = 1 and
    60 ÷ 4 = 15. Then go UP: 7 notebooks: 7 × 15 = 105. 10 notebooks: 10 × 15 =
    150. Check another way: 10 = 4 + 4 + 1 + 1, so 60 + 60 + 15 + 15 = 150. A
    ratio table works for rates too (₹ and notebooks), because the price per
    notebook stays the same. Now you try: 12 notebooks. (₹180)

D3. A painter mixes 15 L of white paint with 10 L of blue paint. How much blue
    paint does he need for 6 L of white paint, keeping the same shade?
        white |   15 |     3 |     6 |
        blue  |   10 |     2 |    __ |
    Answer: 4 L of blue. 15 : 10 = 3 : 2 = 6 : 4.
    Teacher explains: Scale DOWN first to the simplest ratio: divide both by 5:
    15 : 10 = 3 : 2. Now scale UP: 3 × 2 = 6, so 2 × 2 = 4 L of blue. In a ratio
    table the columns are 15 : 10, 3 : 2, 6 : 4. A common mistake is to
    subtract: "15 − 9 = 6, so 10 − 9 = 1 L"; that changes the shade completely.
    Now you try: 9 L of white. (6 L of blue)

D4. Kavya walks 3 km in 40 minutes at a steady pace. Use a double number line to
    find how long she takes to walk 7.5 km.
        km   0      3      6      7.5    9      12
             |------|------|------|------|------|
        min  0      40     80     __     120    160
    Answer: 100 minutes (1 hour 40 minutes).
    Teacher explains: Draw two lines with the same marks: kilometres on top,
    minutes below. Each step is 3 km and 40 min. Mark 3, 6, 9, 12 km above and
    40, 80, 120, 160 min below. 7.5 km is halfway between 6 and 9, so the time
    is halfway between 80 and 120: 100 minutes. Check: 7.5 = 2.5 × 3, and 2.5 ×
    40 = 100. Now you try: 1.5 km. (20 min)

D5. 250 g of cashew nuts cost ₹300. Using a double number line, find the cost of
    1 kg, 750 g and 100 g.
        g    0      250    500    750    1,000
             |------|------|------|------|
        ₹    0      300    600    900    1,200
    Answer: 1 kg ₹1,200, 750 g ₹900, 100 g ₹120.
    Teacher explains: 1 kg = 1000 g = 4 × 250 g, so 1 kg costs 4 × ₹300 =
    ₹1,200. The marks 250, 500, 750, 1000 g match ₹300, ₹600, ₹900, ₹1,200. For
    100 g, go down: 50 g is 250 ÷ 5, so ₹300 ÷ 5 = ₹60, and 100 g is double:
    ₹120. Now you try: 400 g. (₹480)

D6. Asha and Bina share ₹450 in the ratio 2 : 3. How much does each get?
        Asha |  90 |  90 |
        Bina |  90 |  90 |  90 |      5 boxes = ₹450, 1 box = ₹90
    Answer: Asha ₹180, Bina ₹270.
    Teacher explains: The ratio 2 : 3 means that for every ₹2 Asha gets, Bina
    gets ₹3. Draw a bar of 2 + 3 = 5 equal boxes. The whole bar is ₹450, so one
    box is 450 ÷ 5 = ₹90. Asha has 2 boxes: 2 × 90 = ₹180. Bina has 3 boxes: 3 ×
    90 = ₹270. Check: 180 + 270 = 450 and 180 : 270 = 2 : 3 (÷ 90). Now you try:
    share ₹360 in the ratio 4 : 5. (₹160, ₹200)

D7. Rohit shares 56 marbles with his sister in the ratio 3 : 4. He says, "56 ÷ 3
    and 56 ÷ 4". What is wrong? What are the shares?
    Answer: Rohit 24, sister 32.
    Teacher explains: Dividing 56 by 3 and by 4 does not share the marbles out:
    56 ÷ 3 is not even a whole number, and the two answers do not add up to 56.
    The ratio 3 : 4 means 3 + 4 = 7 equal parts. One part is 56 ÷ 7 = 8 marbles.
    Rohit: 3 × 8 = 24; sister: 4 × 8 = 32. Check: 24 + 32 = 56. The number to
    divide by is always the TOTAL number of parts. Now you try: 63 in the ratio
    2 : 7. (14 and 49)

D8. The ratio of boys to girls in a choir is 5 : 4. There are 35 boys. How many
    girls are there? How many children in all?
        boys  | 7 | 7 | 7 | 7 | 7 |   5 parts = 35
        girls | 7 | 7 | 7 | 7 |       4 parts = 28
    Answer: 28 girls, 63 children.
    Teacher explains: Here we know one SHARE, not the total. The boys are 5
    parts and 5 parts = 35, so 1 part = 35 ÷ 5 = 7. Girls are 4 parts: 4 × 7 =
    28. Total: 9 parts = 9 × 7 = 63 (or 35 + 28). A common mistake is to divide
    35 by 9 as if 35 were the total. Read carefully whether the number given is
    a share or the whole. Now you try: ratio 3 : 8 and the smaller share is 24.
    (other 64, total 88)

D9. Money is shared between Rahul and Sohan in the ratio 7 : 3. Rahul gets ₹120
    more than Sohan. How much does each get?
        Rahul | 30 | 30 | 30 | 30 | 30 | 30 | 30 |
        Sohan | 30 | 30 | 30 |<-4 parts = ₹120 ->|
    Answer: Rahul ₹210, Sohan ₹90.
    Teacher explains: Rahul has 7 parts and Sohan 3 parts, so Rahul has 7 − 3 =
    4 parts MORE. Those 4 parts are the extra ₹120, so 1 part = 120 ÷ 4 = ₹30.
    Rahul: 7 × 30 = ₹210; Sohan: 3 × 30 = ₹90. Check: 210 − 90 = 120 and
    210 : 90 = 7 : 3. The bar model shows it clearly: the extra boxes on Rahul's
    bar are the difference. Now you try: ratio 5 : 2, difference 36. (60 and 24)

D10. Grandfather gives ₹1,800 to three grandchildren in the ratio 2 : 3 : 4. How
     much does each get?
     Answer: ₹400, ₹600 and ₹800.
     Teacher explains: A three-part ratio works just like a two-part one. Total
     parts: 2 + 3 + 4 = 9. One part = 1,800 ÷ 9 = ₹200. Shares: 2 × 200 = 400, 3
     × 200 = 600, 4 × 200 = 800. Check: 400 + 600 + 800 = 1,800. Builders use
     three-part ratios too: concrete is often cement : sand : gravel =
     1 : 2 : 4. Now you try: share 90 in the ratio 1 : 2 : 3. (15, 30, 45)

D11. Orange squash is mixed with water in the ratio 1 : 4. How much squash and
     how much water are needed to make 1.5 L of drink?
     Answer: 300 mL of squash and 1.2 L (1,200 mL) of water.
     Teacher explains: The drink is 1 + 4 = 5 parts. Change 1.5 L to 1500 mL to
     avoid decimals. One part = 1500 ÷ 5 = 300 mL. Squash: 1 part = 300 mL.
     Water: 4 parts = 1200 mL = 1.2 L. Check: 300 + 1200 = 1500 mL. Note that
     the squash is 1/5 of the drink, not 1/4: the ratio 1 : 4 is squash to
     water, not squash to drink. Now you try: 2 L of drink with squash : water =
     1 : 3. (500 mL and 1.5 L)

D12. A recipe uses 250 g of flour for 6 eggs. Make a ratio table to find the
     flour for 15 eggs and for 9 eggs.
         eggs  |    6 |     3 |    15 |     9 |
         flour |  250 |   125 |    __ |    __ |
     Answer: 15 eggs: 625 g. 9 eggs: 375 g.
     Teacher explains: 15 is not a whole-number multiple of 6, so find a
     stepping stone. Halve: 3 eggs → 125 g. Now 15 = 5 × 3, so 5 × 125 = 625 g.
     And 9 = 3 × 3, so 3 × 125 = 375 g; or add the columns for 6 and 3: 250 +
     125 = 375. Going to the HCF of the numbers (here 3) is often easier than
     going all the way down to 1 egg (41 2/3 g). Now you try: 21 eggs. (7 × 125
     = 875 g)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Complete each ratio table (scaling up). Answers give the top row / bottom
    row.
    a) |   2 |    4 |    6 |   __ |   20 |
       |   3 |    6 |   __ |   15 |   __ |
    b) |   3 |    6 |   __ |   18 |   30 |
       |   4 |   __ |   16 |   __ |   __ |
    c) |   5 |   15 |   20 |   __ |  100 |
       |   2 |   __ |   __ |   14 |   __ |
    d) |   1 |    2 |    5 |   __ |   12 |
       |   6 |   __ |   __ |   54 |   __ |
    e) |   4 |    8 |   __ |   32 |  100 |
       |   7 |   __ |   21 |   __ |   __ |
    f) |   9 |   18 |   36 |   __ |   __ |
       |   5 |   __ |   __ |   30 |   55 |
    g) |  12 |   36 |   __ |  120 |   __ |
       |   1 |   __ |    5 |   __ |  100 |
    h) |   7 |   __ |   21 |   63 |   __ |
       |   8 |   16 |   __ |   __ |   96 |
    Answers:
      a) 2 | 4 | 6 | 10 | 20  /  3 | 6 | 9 | 15 | 30
      b) 3 | 6 | 12 | 18 | 30  /  4 | 8 | 16 | 24 | 40
      c) 5 | 15 | 20 | 35 | 100  /  2 | 6 | 8 | 14 | 40
      d) 1 | 2 | 5 | 9 | 12  /  6 | 12 | 30 | 54 | 72
      e) 4 | 8 | 12 | 32 | 100  /  7 | 14 | 21 | 56 | 175
      f) 9 | 18 | 36 | 54 | 99  /  5 | 10 | 20 | 30 | 55
      g) 12 | 36 | 60 | 120 | 1,200  /  1 | 3 | 5 | 10 | 100
      h) 7 | 14 | 21 | 63 | 84  /  8 | 16 | 24 | 72 | 96

M2. Complete each ratio table (scale down first, through the HCF or through 1).
    Fill the bottom row.
    a) |   6 |    3 |   15 |    9 |   21 |
       | 250 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    b) |   8 |    4 |    1 |    3 |   10 |
       | 120 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    c) |  10 |    2 |    1 |    7 |   15 |
       |  35 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    d) |  12 |    4 |    2 |   10 |    9 |
       |  30 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    e) |  15 |    5 |    1 |    4 |   10 |
       |  45 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    f) |  20 |    5 |    2 |   12 |   14 |
       |  50 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    g) |   9 |    3 |    6 |   12 |   15 |
       |  12 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    h) |  16 |    4 |    1 |   12 |    2 |
       | 100 |   __ |   __ |   __ |   __ |
    Answers: a) 125, 625, 375, 875   b) 60, 15, 45, 150   c) 7, 3.5, 24.5, 52.5
             d) 10, 5, 25, 22.5   e) 15, 3, 12, 30   f) 12.5, 5, 30, 35
             g) 4, 8, 16, 20   h) 25, 6.25, 75, 12.5

M3. Fill the blanks on each double number line. (The last mark is out of order
    on purpose.)
    a) km   0      3      6      9      12     7.5
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       min  0      40     __     __     __     __
    b) kg   0      1      2      3      4      2.5
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       ₹    0      45     __     __     __     __
    c) m    0      100    200    300    400    50
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       s    0      16     __     __     __     __
    d) L    0      5      10     15     20     1
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       km   0      60     __     __     __     __
    e) pens 0      4      8      12     16     2
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       ₹    0      30     __     __     __     __
    f) h    0      2      4      6      8      3
            |------|------|------|------|------|
       km   0      130    __     __     __     __
    Answers: a) 80, 120, 160, 100 min   b) ₹90, ₹135, ₹180, ₹112.50
             c) 32, 48, 64, 8 s   d) 120, 180, 240, 12 km
             e) ₹60, ₹90, ₹120, ₹15   f) 260, 390, 520, 195 km

M4. Share the number in the given ratio.
    a) 40 in 1 : 3    b) 450 in 2 : 3     c) 56 in 3 : 4   d) 360 in 4 : 5
    e) 63 in 2 : 7    f) 100 in 1 : 4     g) 90 in 1 : 2   h) 72 in 5 : 3
    i) 120 in 7 : 5   j) 48 in 1 : 1      k) 81 in 4 : 5   l) 200 in 3 : 7
    m) 150 in 2 : 1   n) 99 in 5 : 6      o) 64 in 3 : 5   p) 500 in 1 : 9
    q) 36 in 7 : 2    r) 1,000 in 3 : 2   s) 84 in 5 : 7   t) 240 in 11 : 1
    Answers: a) 10, 30   b) 180, 270   c) 24, 32   d) 160, 200   e) 14, 49
             f) 20, 80   g) 30, 60   h) 45, 27   i) 70, 50   j) 24, 24
             k) 36, 45   l) 60, 140   m) 100, 50   n) 45, 54   o) 24, 40
             p) 50, 450   q) 28, 8   r) 600, 400   s) 35, 49   t) 220, 20

M5. Share the amount of rupees in the given ratio.
    a) ₹1,800 in 5 : 4     b) ₹2,400 in 3 : 5    c) ₹7,500 in 7 : 8
    d) ₹1,350 in 4 : 5     e) ₹6,000 in 9 : 11   f) ₹945 in 2 : 3
    g) ₹1,080 in 5 : 7     h) ₹3,150 in 4 : 3    i) ₹12,000 in 3 : 1
    j) ₹4,200 in 8 : 13    k) ₹990 in 4 : 7      l) ₹5,600 in 9 : 5
    m) ₹2,250 in 2 : 7     n) ₹8,800 in 5 : 6    o) ₹1,440 in 7 : 5
    p) ₹10,000 in 13 : 7
    Answers: a) ₹1,000, ₹800   b) ₹900, ₹1,500   c) ₹3,500, ₹4,000
             d) ₹600, ₹750   e) ₹2,700, ₹3,300   f) ₹378, ₹567   g) ₹450, ₹630
             h) ₹1,800, ₹1,350   i) ₹9,000, ₹3,000   j) ₹1,600, ₹2,600
             k) ₹360, ₹630   l) ₹3,600, ₹2,000   m) ₹500, ₹1,750
             n) ₹4,000, ₹4,800   o) ₹840, ₹600   p) ₹6,500, ₹3,500

M6. Share in the ratio. Give the answers in the smaller unit shown.
    a) 1 m in 2 : 3 (cm)     b) 2 kg in 3 : 5 (g)     c) 1 h in 7 : 5 (min)
    d) ₹10 in 1 : 4 (₹)      e) 4.5 L in 1 : 2 (mL)   f) 1.2 km in 5 : 1 (m)
    g) ₹25 in 3 : 2 (₹)      h) 3 m in 1 : 5 (cm)     i) 1 day in 5 : 3 (h)
    j) 1.5 kg in 7 : 3 (g)   k) ₹7.50 in 2 : 1 (₹)    l) 2 L in 1 : 3 (mL)
    Answers: a) 40 cm, 60 cm   b) 750 g, 1,250 g   c) 35 min, 25 min   d) ₹2, ₹8
             e) 1,500 mL, 3,000 mL   f) 1,000 m, 200 m   g) ₹15, ₹10
             h) 50 cm, 250 cm   i) 15 h, 9 h   j) 1,050 g, 450 g   k) ₹5, ₹2.50
             l) 500 mL, 1,500 mL

M7. One share is given. Find the other share and the total.
    a) x : y = 3 : 5, x = 12    b) x : y = 5 : 4, x = 35
    c) x : y = 3 : 8, x = 24    d) x : y = 2 : 7, x = 14
    e) x : y = 4 : 9, y = 36    f) x : y = 7 : 3, y = 21
    g) x : y = 1 : 6, x = 8     h) x : y = 5 : 2, y = 20
    i) x : y = 11 : 4, x = 44   j) x : y = 2 : 3, y = 150
    k) x : y = 6 : 5, x = 42    l) x : y = 9 : 10, y = 90
    m) x : y = 3 : 1, x = 45    n) x : y = 8 : 3, y = 12
    o) x : y = 5 : 12, y = 60   p) x : y = 4 : 7, x = 100
    Answers: a) y = 20, total = 32   b) y = 28, total = 63
             c) y = 64, total = 88   d) y = 49, total = 63
             e) x = 16, total = 52   f) x = 49, total = 70
             g) y = 48, total = 56   h) x = 50, total = 70
             i) y = 16, total = 60   j) x = 100, total = 250
             k) y = 35, total = 77   l) x = 81, total = 171
             m) y = 15, total = 60   n) x = 32, total = 44
             o) x = 25, total = 85   p) y = 175, total = 275

M8. The difference between the shares is given. Find both shares.
    a) x : y = 7 : 3, difference 120   b) x : y = 5 : 2, difference 36
    c) x : y = 4 : 1, difference 45    d) x : y = 9 : 5, difference 28
    e) x : y = 3 : 8, difference 40    f) x : y = 2 : 5, difference 12
    g) x : y = 11 : 7, difference 20   h) x : y = 6 : 5, difference 7
    i) x : y = 1 : 4, difference 60    j) x : y = 10 : 3, difference 49
    k) x : y = 5 : 9, difference 100   l) x : y = 13 : 8, difference 25
    Answers: a) x = 210, y = 90   b) x = 60, y = 24   c) x = 60, y = 15
             d) x = 63, y = 35   e) x = 24, y = 64   f) x = 8, y = 20
             g) x = 55, y = 35   h) x = 42, y = 35   i) x = 20, y = 80
             j) x = 70, y = 21   k) x = 125, y = 225   l) x = 65, y = 40

M9. Share in the three-part ratio.
    a) 1,800 in 2 : 3 : 4   b) 90 in 1 : 2 : 3      c) 35 in 1 : 2 : 4
    d) 120 in 3 : 4 : 5     e) 1,000 in 1 : 1 : 2   f) 720 in 5 : 3 : 1
    g) 660 in 2 : 4 : 5     h) 1,500 in 7 : 5 : 3   i) 84 in 1 : 1 : 1
    j) 2,400 in 3 : 5 : 8   k) 390 in 6 : 4 : 3     l) 5,000 in 1 : 3 : 6
    Answers: a) 400, 600, 800   b) 15, 30, 45   c) 5, 10, 20   d) 30, 40, 50
             e) 250, 250, 500   f) 400, 240, 80   g) 120, 240, 300
             h) 700, 500, 300   i) 28, 28, 28   j) 450, 750, 1,200
             k) 180, 120, 90   l) 500, 1,500, 3,000

M10. a) to j): write in simplest form. k) to p): find the other two shares and
     the total.
     a) 10 : 15 : 20                    b) 6 : 12 : 18
     c) 8 : 4 : 12                      d) 25 : 50 : 75
     e) 14 : 21 : 35                    f) 9 : 6 : 3
     g) 100 : 250 : 150                 h) 0.5 : 1 : 1.5
     i) 12 : 18 : 30                    j) 40 : 24 : 16
     k) x : y : z = 2 : 3 : 4, x = 10   l) x : y : z = 1 : 2 : 4, z = 20
     m) x : y : z = 3 : 5 : 7, y = 25   n) x : y : z = 4 : 1 : 3, x = 36
     o) x : y : z = 2 : 5 : 6, z = 42   p) x : y : z = 1 : 3 : 5, y = 12
     Answers: a) 2 : 3 : 4   b) 1 : 2 : 3   c) 2 : 1 : 3   d) 1 : 2 : 3
              e) 2 : 3 : 5   f) 3 : 2 : 1   g) 2 : 5 : 3   h) 1 : 2 : 3
              i) 2 : 3 : 5   j) 5 : 3 : 2   k) y = 15, z = 20, total = 45
              l) x = 5, y = 10, total = 35   m) x = 15, z = 35, total = 75
              n) y = 9, z = 27, total = 72   o) x = 14, y = 35, total = 91
              p) x = 4, z = 20, total = 36

M11. What fraction of the total is x? Then find x and y.
     a) x : y = 3 : 5, x + y = 64    b) x : y = 2 : 3, x + y = 45
     c) x : y = 1 : 4, x + y = 30    d) x : y = 5 : 7, x + y = 96
     e) x : y = 4 : 5, x + y = 27    f) x : y = 7 : 1, x + y = 48
     g) x : y = 3 : 7, x + y = 250   h) x : y = 5 : 3, x + y = 1.6
     i) x : y = 2 : 9, x + y = 55    j) x : y = 1 : 1, x + y = 17
     k) x : y = 9 : 7, x + y = 800   l) x : y = 6 : 5, x + y = 132
     Answers:
       a) x = 3/8 of total = 24, y = 40
       b) x = 2/5 of total = 18, y = 27
       c) x = 1/5 of total = 6, y = 24
       d) x = 5/12 of total = 40, y = 56
       e) x = 4/9 of total = 12, y = 15
       f) x = 7/8 of total = 42, y = 6
       g) x = 3/10 of total = 75, y = 175
       h) x = 5/8 of total = 1, y = 0.6
       i) x = 2/11 of total = 10, y = 45
       j) x = 1/2 of total = 8.5, y = 8.5
       k) x = 9/16 of total = 450, y = 350
       l) x = 6/11 of total = 72, y = 60

M12. A number was shared into these two shares. In what ratio (simplest form)
     was it shared?
     a) 72 → 32 and 40      b) 100 → 35 and 65   c) 60 → 24 and 36
     d) 45 → 15 and 30      e) 90 → 54 and 36    f) 1,000 → 375 and 625
     g) 84 → 12 and 72      h) 150 → 90 and 60   i) 200 → 120 and 80
     j) 56 → 21 and 35      k) 120 → 50 and 70   l) 64 → 40 and 24
     m) 360 → 135 and 225   n) 99 → 44 and 55    o) 500 → 100 and 400
     p) 48 → 18 and 30
     Answers: a) 4 : 5   b) 7 : 13   c) 2 : 3   d) 1 : 2   e) 3 : 2   f) 3 : 5
              g) 1 : 6   h) 3 : 2   i) 3 : 2   j) 3 : 5   k) 5 : 7   l) 5 : 3
              m) 3 : 5   n) 4 : 5   o) 1 : 4   p) 3 : 5

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: act out sharing with real coins or counters, dealing "2 to you, 3
  to me" in rounds, then count the rounds; always draw the bar model with equal
  boxes; keep ratio tables to whole-number multipliers first. Harder: combine
  two ratios (a : b = 2 : 3 and b : c = 4 : 5 give a : b : c = 8 : 12 : 15),
  problems where the ratio changes after someone gives some away, mixing paints
  or drinks to a target strength, and sharing a prize when the totals are given
  in different units.


================================================================================
TOPIC 5.13 — UNITARY METHOD
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - The UNITARY METHOD: first find the value of ONE unit, then multiply to find
    the value of MANY units. ("Unit" means one.)
  - Write it in three sentences: "5 pens cost ₹60. 1 pen costs ₹60 ÷ 5 = ₹12. 8
    pens cost ₹12 × 8 = ₹96."
  - DIRECT PROPORTION: more of one means more of the other in the same ratio
    (more pens, more cost; more litres, more kilometres). Divide to find one,
    multiply to find many.
  - If the value of one unit is an awkward number, multiply first and divide
    last: 6 kg cost ₹500, so 15 kg cost 500 × 15 ÷ 6 = ₹1,250.
  - INVERSE PROPORTION (introduction): more of one means LESS of the other (more
    workers, fewer days; faster speed, less time). The total work stays the
    same: 6 workers × 10 days = 60 worker-days, so 15 workers need 60 ÷ 15 = 4
    days. For ONE unit, MULTIPLY; for many, DIVIDE.
  - Before calculating, ask: "If this goes up, does the answer go up or down?"
    Some things are neither (a child's age and height).
  - Time and work (lightly): if a job takes 6 hours, 1/6 of it is done in 1
    hour, and 4/6 = 2/3 in 4 hours.
  - Check that the answer is sensible, and use it for recipes, wages, speed,
    fuel and prices in ₹.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. 5 pens cost ₹60. What is the cost of 8 pens?
        5 pens → ₹60
        1 pen  → ₹60 ÷ 5 = ₹12
        8 pens → ₹12 × 8 = ₹96
    Answer: ₹96.
    Teacher explains: Step 1, find the cost of ONE pen: 5 pens cost ₹60, so 1
    pen costs ₹60 ÷ 5 = ₹12 (fewer pens, less money: divide). Step 2, find the
    cost of MANY: 8 pens cost 8 × ₹12 = ₹96 (more pens, more money: multiply).
    Write the working in sentences, keeping the thing we are counting (pens) on
    the left and the thing we want (₹) on the right. Check: 8 pens is more than
    5, so the cost must be more than ₹60. Now you try: 7 notebooks cost ₹245.
    What do 12 cost? (₹35 each, ₹420)

D2. A dozen bananas cost ₹54. How much do 20 bananas cost?
    Answer: ₹90.
    Teacher explains: A dozen is 12. 1 banana costs ₹54 ÷ 12 = ₹4.50 (54 ÷ 12 =
    4.5, and in money we write ₹4.50). 20 bananas cost 20 × ₹4.50 = ₹90. The
    unit price need not be a whole number: 4.5 × 20 = 90. Check: 20 is a little
    less than 2 dozen, and 2 dozen would cost ₹108, so ₹90 is sensible. Now you
    try: 8 eggs cost ₹52. What do 15 eggs cost? (₹6.50 each, ₹97.50)

D3. Kheer for 4 people needs 1 L of milk, 120 g of rice and 150 g of sugar. How
    much of each is needed for 6 people?
    Answer: 1.5 L of milk, 180 g of rice and 225 g of sugar.
    Teacher explains: Find the amounts for ONE person by dividing by 4: milk 1 ÷
    4 = 0.25 L, rice 120 ÷ 4 = 30 g, sugar 150 ÷ 4 = 37.5 g. Then multiply by 6:
    milk 0.25 × 6 = 1.5 L, rice 30 × 6 = 180 g, sugar 37.5 × 6 = 225 g.
    Shortcut: 6 people = 1.5 × 4 people, so multiply everything by 1.5. Every
    ingredient is scaled by the SAME number; adding 2 of everything would spoil
    the kheer. Now you try: for 10 people. (2.5 L, 300 g, 375 g)

D4. A car goes 150 km on 10 litres of petrol. How far does it go on 16 litres?
    How much petrol does it need for 405 km?
    Answer: 240 km on 16 L. 27 L for 405 km.
    Teacher explains: Unit rate: on 1 litre the car goes 150 ÷ 10 = 15 km. On 16
    litres: 15 × 16 = 240 km. In the second question the unknown is the LITRES.
    Each litre covers 15 km, so count how many lots of 15 km fit into 405 km:
    405 ÷ 15 = 27 litres. Check: 27 × 15 = 405. Always decide what "one unit"
    should be: 1 litre here. Now you try: 360 km? (24 L)

D5. A mason earns ₹2,400 for 6 days of work. How much does he earn in 25 days at
    the same rate?
    Answer: ₹10,000.
    Teacher explains: 1 day: ₹2,400 ÷ 6 = ₹400. 25 days: ₹400 × 25 = ₹10,000.
    This is direct proportion: twice as many days, twice the money. A quick
    check: 24 days is 4 × 6 days, so ₹2,400 × 4 = ₹9,600, and one more day is
    ₹400, total ₹10,000. Now you try: ₹3,150 for 7 days; how much for 30 days?
    (₹450 a day, ₹13,500)

D6. 6 kg of dry fruit cost ₹500. What do 15 kg cost?
    Answer: ₹1,250.
    Teacher explains: The cost of 1 kg is 500 ÷ 6 = 83 1/3 rupees, an awkward
    number. So MULTIPLY FIRST, then divide: cost of 15 kg = 500 × 15 ÷ 6 = 7,500
    ÷ 6 = ₹1,250. The unitary idea is the same (divide by 6 for one kg, multiply
    by 15), we just do the steps in the other order. Another way: 15 kg = 2.5 ×
    6 kg, so ₹500 × 2.5 = ₹1,250. Now you try: 9 m of cloth cost ₹600; what do
    15 m cost? (600 × 15 ÷ 9 = ₹1,000)

D7. A train travels 270 km in 3 hours at a steady speed. How far does it go in 5
    hours? How long does it take for 630 km?
    Answer: 450 km in 5 hours. 7 hours for 630 km.
    Teacher explains: In 1 hour: 270 ÷ 3 = 90 km (the speed, 90 km/h). In 5
    hours: 90 × 5 = 450 km. For 630 km: each hour covers 90 km, so the number of
    hours is 630 ÷ 90 = 7. Check: 7 × 90 = 630. The same unit rate (90 km per
    hour) answers both questions: multiply to find distance, divide to find
    time. (More in 5.31, speed-distance-time.) Now you try: a bus goes 120 km in
    2 hours; how far in 3.5 hours? (210 km)

D8. 6 workers can build a wall in 10 days. How long would 15 workers take,
    working at the same rate?
        6 workers  → 10 days
        1 worker   → 10 × 6 = 60 days
        15 workers → 60 ÷ 15 = 4 days
    Answer: 4 days.
    Teacher explains: Think: MORE workers finish in LESS time, so this is
    inverse proportion; we must not multiply the days by 15/6. Step 1, one
    worker: 1 worker would take 6 times as long: 10 × 6 = 60 days (the wall is
    60 worker-days of work). Step 2, 15 workers share those 60 worker-days: 60 ÷
    15 = 4 days. Notice the operations are the OPPOSITE of direct proportion:
    multiply to go to one, divide to go to many. Check: 6 × 10 = 60 and 15 × 4 =
    60. Now you try: 8 taps fill a tank in 9 hours; how long do 12 taps take?
    (72 ÷ 12 = 6 hours)

D9. Direct, inverse or neither? (a) the number of kg of rice and its cost; (b)
    the speed of a car and the time for a 100 km trip; (c) the number of
    children sharing 60 sweets and the sweets each gets; (d) a child's age and
    height.
    Answer: (a) direct, (b) inverse, (c) inverse, (d) neither.
    Teacher explains: Ask: when the first doubles, what happens to the second?
    (a) Twice the rice costs twice as much: direct. (b) Twice the speed takes
    half the time: inverse (speed × time = 100 stays the same). (c) Twice as
    many children get half as many sweets each: inverse (children × sweets each
    = 60). (d) A child of 10 is not twice as tall as a child of 5: neither, so
    the unitary method does NOT apply. Always check this before calculating. Now
    you try: the number of pages printed and the time taken by one printer.
    (direct)

D10. A hostel has food for 120 students for 30 days. If 150 students come, how
     long will the food last?
     Answer: 24 days.
     Teacher explains: More students eat the food faster, so it lasts FEWER
     days: inverse proportion. The food is 120 × 30 = 3,600 student-days. For 1
     student it would last 3,600 days; for 150 students it lasts 3,600 ÷ 150 =
     24 days. Check: 24 is less than 30, as expected. A common mistake is 30 ×
     150 ÷ 120 = 37.5 days, which says more students make the food last longer.
     Now you try: fodder for 60 cows lasts 20 days; how long for 80 cows? (15
     days)

D11. Ravi can paint a fence in 6 hours. What fraction of the fence does he paint
     in 1 hour? In 4 hours?
     Answer: 1/6 in 1 hour. 4/6 = 2/3 in 4 hours.
     Teacher explains: The whole job takes 6 hours, so in each hour he does 1 of
     6 equal parts: 1/6 of the fence. This is the unitary method for work: find
     the work done in ONE hour. In 4 hours he does 4 × 1/6 = 4/6 = 2/3. The part
     still to do is 1 − 2/3 = 1/3, which needs 2 more hours. Now you try: a tank
     fills in 8 minutes. What fraction fills in 3 minutes? (3/8)

D12. 5 machines make 400 bottles in 4 hours. How many bottles do 3 machines make
     in 6 hours?
         5 machines, 4 h → 400
         1 machine,  4 h → 400 ÷ 5 = 80
         1 machine,  1 h → 80 ÷ 4 = 20
         3 machines, 1 h → 20 × 3 = 60
         3 machines, 6 h → 60 × 6 = 360
     Answer: 360 bottles.
     Teacher explains: Two things change, so go to ONE machine for ONE hour. 5
     machines in 4 hours: 400. 1 machine in 4 hours: 400 ÷ 5 = 80. 1 machine in
     1 hour: 80 ÷ 4 = 20 bottles. Now build up: 3 machines in 1 hour: 3 × 20 =
     60; 3 machines in 6 hours: 60 × 6 = 360. Both changes are direct here (more
     machines, more bottles; more hours, more bottles). Now you try: 4 machines
     make 480 in 3 hours; how many do 2 machines make in 5 hours? (400)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Find the value of ONE unit. (n → ₹p means n things cost ₹p.)
    a) 5 → ₹60; 1 → __      b) 4 → ₹48; 1 → __     c) 3 → ₹36; 1 → __
    d) 6 → ₹90; 1 → __      e) 8 → ₹120; 1 → __    f) 10 → ₹250; 1 → __
    g) 7 → ₹245; 1 → __     h) 12 → ₹54; 1 → __    i) 9 → ₹81; 1 → __

M2. Find the value of the second amount (direct proportion).

    p) 6 → ₹3; 9 → __        q) 15 → ₹225; 4 → __   r) 2 → ₹13; 7 → __

    Answers: a) ₹96   b) ₹84   c) ₹120   d) ₹60   e) ₹45   f) ₹300   g) ₹420

             n) ₹275   o) ₹1,600   p) ₹4.50   q) ₹60   r) ₹45.50   s) ₹105
             t) ₹60

M3. The value of one unit is a decimal. Find the second amount.
    a) 12 → ₹54; 20 → __    b) 8 → ₹52; 15 → __     c) 4 → ₹18; 9 → __
    d) 6 → ₹27; 10 → __     e) 5 → ₹17.50; 8 → __   f) 10 → ₹45; 7 → __
    g) 20 → ₹130; 3 → __    h) 8 → ₹36; 5 → __      i) 16 → ₹60; 12 → __
    j) 2 → ₹7; 9 → __       k) 4 → ₹5; 14 → __      l) 50 → ₹75; 30 → __
    m) 3 → ₹4.50; 11 → __   n) 40 → ₹90; 6 → __     o) 6 → ₹15; 5 → __
